ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u1	Resume session. I would like to call on Adam Andruszkiewicz, Marcin Duszek, Krzysztof Kubow and Arkadiusz Marchewka to be the secretaries of today's proceedings. The first part will be attended by Marcin Duszek and Krzysztof Kubów. The minutes and the list of speakers will be led by Marcin Souszek and Arkadiusz Carrot. We proceed with the examination of item 18 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on VAT on goods and services and some other laws (print No 965). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Leszek Skiba to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u2	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending the law on value added tax and certain other laws is a government project. In addition to the amendment of the law on tax on goods and services, this proposal also provides for amendments to the tax Code and Tax Order. The proposed solutions represent a further step towards the recovery of VAT revenues. The aim of the proposed solutions is to seal the VAT system, to ensure greater stability of VAT receipts, to restore fair competition on the market by eliminating unfair operators from trading. In addition, the project includes provisions allowing a wide range of businesses to benefit from the VAT exemption. It is proposed to increase the turnover limit for sales eligible for exemption from 150 000. PLN to 200,000. zł. Small businesses, when exempt, are not required to issue VAT invoices, to settle taxes or to keep detailed sales records. The proposed solution is a simplification of the settlement of the smallest enterprises. The Act also provides for amendments aimed at bringing the provisions of the Act on value added tax more fully into line with the provisions of Directive 2006/12/EC as regards tax exemption, inter alia. insurance services in connection with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 17 March 2016 in Case C-40/15 Aspiro SA. The proposed provisions also complement the regulation on centralisation of VAT settlements in local government units. In view of the fact that entities whose activities are not related to business activities may register for VAT purposes, but are geared towards VAT fraud by placing empty invoices on the market, it is necessary to identify them as soon as possible, prevent them from registering or quickly registering taxable persons. Therefore, the draft provides for the introduction of provisions enabling the registration of such entities to be refused after verification of the information contained in their registration. In view of the importance of keeping the VAT register as current as possible in terms of both the protection of the interests of the state budget and the protection of honest taxpayers for whom the register is the basis for the first verification of their counterparties, the draft lists situations in which tax authorities will remove from the register entities which should not be included in it, either because of the actual cessation of the activities taxed or because of the activities which are difficult to assign to economic activities. In addition, the draft decided in a decisive manner to remove from the VAT register the entities issuing the so-called "taxpayers'. empty invoices and consciously involved in fraud or tax fraud aimed at obtaining undue property benefits at the expense of the Treasury. To prevent the recording of so-called. the posts by the agents were proposed to impose joint and several liability on the representatives of the entity starting the business. The liability of the representative would concern the tax arrears arising from the activities carried out during the first half of the year for registration and would be limited to cases where they result from the unfair settlement of VAT in order to confer a financial advantage, i.e. tax fraud. The proxy would respond jointly and severally to the amount of 500,000. zł. The project provides for the elimination of quarterly settlements for large taxpayers and those who start carrying out activities taxed for the first year. This solution will provide timely information to the tax authorities and will end the use of unconsolidated settlement periods for the seller and buyer in tax fraud-oriented activities. In addition, it will translate into the stability of the monthly revenue of the state budget. Accelerating the analysis of fiscal data will result in an obligation to submit VAT declarations only by electronic means. This is the case in stages: from 1 January, taxpayers making intra-Community transactions will be required to supply national supplies or purchase in reverse charge systems and those who are required under separate rules to submit documents, including. settlement declarations by electronic means. The remaining taxpayers will be covered by this obligation on 1 January 2018. The summary information submitted by taxable persons carrying out intra-Community transactions and subject to the reverse charge regime will also be submitted only for monthly periods and sent exclusively by electronic means. Another element of the sealing package is the clarification of the VAT refund rules. The project provides for additional conditions for receipt of reimbursement on an accelerated 25-day deadline. This will only be possible for taxpayers whose credibility could already be verified, i.e. registered for at least one year and making tax returns during that period. In view of the operational arrangements of the competent services, the time limit may also be extended to 3 months when the Chief of Police, Head of the Central Anti-corruption Office, Head of the Internal Security Agency or Prosecutor General makes such a request in connection with the proceedings. The draft law proposed to the House proposes to extend the application of the reverse charge mechanism to further goods. This is one of the important instruments aimed at combating VAT fraud, whose effectiveness has been confirmed both by the analyses carried out and by the control activities carried out. The tax in this mechanism is settled by the buyer, not by the seller. In view of the irregularities observed in the market for construction services in the field of VAT, including by means of invoices which do not reflect the actual transaction, it is proposed to include a substantial part of the transactions in the area of construction services. In addition, due to the increasing problem of fraud in the market so-called. electronics has been proposed to include this instrument of transactions which are the subject of processors, and it is proposed here to provide for a functioning solution for other electronics goods such as portable computers, mobile phones, gaming consoles, setting a quota limit of 20,000. zł for the application of this measure. At the same time, the proposal provides for a comprehensive regulation of the rules on the taxation of goods in the categories of gold, silver and platinum already partly covered by the reverse charge mechanism and partly by the institution of joint and several liability. For these goods, the reverse charge mechanism will be settled. As regards joint and several liability institutions, it is proposed to extend it to transactions involving stretch film and hard drives. The proposed bill proposes to modify the institution of joint and several liability and the guarantee deposit in order to make it more effective. The additional conditions that must apply for the purchaser to be freed from joint and several liability for the seller's tax obligations are the compulsory payment without cash and, in the case of fuels, purchase them from entities licensed under the relevant concession required in the performance of that activity. Since one of the conditions excluding joint and several liability of the purchaser is the acquisition of goods from a taxable person who has lodged a guarantee deposit, it is important that the list includes entities guaranteeing that his tax obligations can be regulated. Therefore, a seller who is in the course of restructuring, insolvency or liquidation proceedings cannot be notified to the list of entities. In addition, such entity must authorise the bank or cooperative credit and savings bank, which maintains the account indicated in the identification declaration, to provide tax authorities or tax control authorities with information on all transactions made in that account. Another solution aimed at eliminating VAT is the introduction of tax sanctions for incorrect VAT clearance. The project assumes that the control authority will determine the amount of this obligation in the correct amount and will set an additional tax liability of 30%. the amounts of irregularities found. Tax fraud will be treated with all severity, including the so-called issue of tax fraud. empty invoices. The penalty applied in this case is increased to 100%. It should be noted that the sanctioning institution is not a new solution and has already operated in the VAT Act until 30 November 2008. It is primarily preventive and seeks to convince taxpayers that it is in their interest to properly and diligently fill in tax returns and to stress that attempts to commit fraud by means of an empty invoice will be severely punished. In addition to the above-mentioned VAT sealing and penalising arrangements carried out by taxable persons, the amendments provided for in the Tax Criminal Code concerning the tightening of sanctions for persons involved in tax fraud should be added. In particular, it is proposed that sanctions be imposed on specific situations where a person puts up so-called. empty invoices or use them to deduct tax. This act would also be punishable by imprisonment. It should be noted here that when the issuer of the invoice is so-called. pole, i.e. an entity that has virtually no assets, imposing a financial penalty on it does not have the desired effect because it cannot be enforced. However, such entities must not remain unpunished. The tightening of sanctions is primarily preventive and is intended to discourage participation in this activity. The proposed amendments to the Tax Order are a consequence of the amendment to the VAT Act and provide for the non-application of an increased rate of interest for tax arrears when VAT has already imposed a tax sanction. In addition, the changes in the Tax Order are linked to the extension of the institution of joint and several liability to the taxable person's agent whose registration has been filed by him, and to the addition of a provision allowing verification of the correctness of the refund of the tax difference not only through verification of the taxpayer's accounts, but also by verifying the accounts of other taxable persons involved in the trading of the goods or services which are the subject of the taxpayer's settlement. The proposed changes are also due to the elimination of taxpayers' ability to settle on goods and services other than small taxpayers for quarterly periods. The introduction of the measures discussed to increase the effectiveness of the fight against fraud should lead to a faster elimination from the economic market of operators who are not willing to pay or refund VAT due. This is expected, above all, by honest entrepreneurs who are severely affected by unfair competition. These actions will improve the business environment of honest taxpayers. The proposed measures to improve VAT collection are expected to reduce the tax gap by 2 percentage points, resulting in a positive annual budgetary impact of PLN 3400 million. Given that the proposed sealing solutions will only produce results in the following years, the proposed project proposes to maintain the current rate of VAT at 8% and 23% until 31 December 2018 The scale of fraud and its harm to honest entrepreneurs in the economy of the state budget are in favour of introducing the proposed solutions as soon as possible. The draft law indicates here the date of 1 January 2017. The entry into force of the law at the beginning of the year also calls for the possibility of covering a wider group of small businesses with VAT exemption as soon as possible as a result of an increase in the turnover limit for this exemption. On the other hand, the entry into force of the regulations supplementing the centralisation of VAT in local government units is foreseen at 1 January 2018. In presenting the above, I kindly ask the High House to refer this proposal to further legislative work. Thank you very much.

I present the opinion of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the draft law on the amendment of the law on goods and services and among others, contained in print No. 965. amendments to the Tax Code Act. The aim of this project is primarily to seal the VAT collection system and protect the Polish state from losses caused by loss of influence from this tax as a result of its extortion on a huge scale. Experts are talking about the scale of fraud of 50 billion a year, which represents about 1/3 of all the proceeds from this tax. VAT is the largest single source of budgetary revenue and the loss of revenue caused by fraud is equal to or even greater than the budget deficit. If it had not been uncontrolled during the previous term, under the rule of the Civic Platform, the increase in VAT fraud, we would have had a situation that would have brought us closer to a balanced state budget at the moment. Already in 2016, due to the first legislative decisions related to the sealing of VAT collection, there was a significant increase in revenue from this tax compared to one year to one year. These actions have shown that unfair practices can be effectively countered, acting within the framework of European law and without prejudice to small and medium-sized honest taxpayers. This will also be the result of work on the project submitted by the government. It is worth mentioning that a tight and equal tax system, which is therefore a fair tax system, is a lower burden for citizens and fair competition for companies. Moreover, on behalf of the government, you made a kind of undertaking in this case, saying that, in view of higher VAT revenues, it would be possible in the future to return to the previous lower rates of that tax. Of the proposed solutions, the proposal to extend the so-called system deserves attention. reverse VAT burden on those goods and services, which are particularly vulnerable to fraud, often taking a form of large-scale organised and millions of zlotys. An important element here is the use of column companies, non-asset companies, which form a chain of transactions and a system of so-called. empty invoices. The project proposes e.g. extension of Annex 11 to new types of goods and services, supplementing the list covered by the reverse charge. This extension includes e.g. Unwrought silver or in semi-finished form, in primary forms or in semi-finished form, of a sample of less than 0,325 , similarly, stone jewelry with a sample of less than 0,325 , similarly platinum with the same ww. properties as well as processors used in electronic equipment. It is worth noting that CPU transactions will only be subject to a reverse charge on VAT when the limit of 20,000 is exceeded. zł. This approach shows that the government takes into account the demands and needs of smaller taxpayers in the project. The same applies to this element of the Act, which concerns an increase in the limit on VAT-free turnover. The same applies to the system of penalties provided for in the Tax Code. Here, the very severe consequences relate to transactions that amount to millions of zlotys, with the possibility of joint and several liability and relief of penalties for cooperation with state authorities in the detection of members of the crime in order to break down the solidarity of organised crime. In addition to the abovementioned extension of Annex 11 we will also work on the new text of Annex 14, to which greenhouse gas emission allowances services are transferred, with the addition of certain categories of works, where a lot of fraud has also been detected - this is also the experience of fiscal controls. The catalogue of Annexes 11 and 14 is extended precisely as a result of the experience of the State's tax services in relation to the observed transfer practices in the field of VAT fraud from the sectors covered by the reverse VAT mechanism. Another group of proposed amendments concerns taking into account the case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union regarding VAT exemptions. According to that case law, the exemption from financial services cannot be granted separately to support services of a purely technical nature, such as administrative services. or call center. During the detailed work on the law in the finance committee, we declare a thorough examination of all the doubts regarding the application of the new rules to [[♪ The bell ♪]] small and medium-sized companies honestly fulfilling their obligations. I'm almost done, Marshal. In conclusion, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I support a government project aimed at sealing the VAT collection system, hoping that this will significantly reduce the budget deficit and consequently reduce the tax burden on citizens. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u6	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform, to present a position on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Taxation on Goods and Services and certain other laws. The proposed law is very important due to the wide range of regulations, as well as the purpose of sealing the collection system for goods and services. The scale of the fraud and the size of the so-called fraud. The gaps in the value added tax are worrying enough to require further legislative intervention. However, when trying to combat fraud, to reduce the gap, a child cannot be poured out with a bath, it must not be forgotten that the tax on goods and services is a consumer tax that should remain neutral for entrepreneurs. Entrepreneurs should not charge either the amount of tax itself or excessive compliance costs or risks. It is therefore necessary to modify a number of project solutions precisely in order to balance the interests of taxpayers and the state budget accordingly. I would like to say right away that a number of provisions deserve a positive assessment. I have no doubt about that. But Mrs Bubula spoke of them, releasing me from this obligation. So I'm going to talk about reservations. Serious objections are raised by the introduction of joint and several liability of the representative representing the taxable person in the course of the registration. After all, fraudsters will continue to obtain VAT registration using poles, but acting personally in the course of the registration. Taxpayers, including those who are honest, will practically be deprived of the possibility of professional legal assistance during registration. Agents, without being able to verify the potential client's intentions, will not take risks. The so-called VAT sanction or additional liability will be a solution that significantly increases the risk on the part of taxpayers, especially since it can lead to even a one- or several-day delay in the submission of a declaration and payment of a tax or an unconscious error in the settlement, which is not difficult at the same time to pay such a complex tax. Although the additional commitment will not be determined in the case of accounting errors, the errors resulting from e.g. from discrepancies in judicial or administrative case law. Such a measure of liability for undesired errors could be justified if we consider that the Court of Justice draws attention to the importance of good faith or due diligence in the common VAT system. The removal from the taxable person's register will also be a sanction for those who have been victims of fraud. Should a one-off involvement in a transaction, which has proved to be part of a fraud but committed by another entity, actually lead to the removal from the register of active VAT taxable persons of an entity which, moreover, conducts a fair business activity? This solution seems too far-reaching, especially if we consider the great indeterminateness of good faith or due diligence in making sure that we are not dealing with a fraud, and the indeterminateness of the notion of an unreliable counterparty in tax settlement. The removal from the register of taxable persons who have not carried out activities taxed for 6 months, and subsequently the restoration of those activities, when it turns out that the absence of taxable activities during a given period is due to the specific nature of the activity, may be a solution which causes excessive practical difficulties. It should also be specified that the deletion is subject to an entity whose proxy is not repeated at the request of the Authority so that a one-off event does not lead to deletion from the register. Both the additional obligation, the removal from the VAT register and the criminal sanctions will primarily affect the victims of frauds and not the organisers of fraudulent activities. For issuing an empty invoice and using it will be answered by the exhibitor and the recipient, but no longer by the entity who organized the fraudulent activity, hiding behind the pillar, directing fraudulent activities and profiting from it. Of course, increasing the responsibility of the pillars and counterparties is intended to discourage them from properly companying the activities of actual fraudsters and entering into commercial relations with suspicious counterparties, which may hinder the activities of fraudsters and reduce the scale of fraud. [[♪ The bell ♪]] However, the responsibility of real fraudsters, organizers of fraudulent structures seems inadequately regulated. This is also not a good solution for the 23 percent rate.

In addition, the project amends the rules on the settlement of VAT within a 25-day period, limits the possibility of quarterly settlements, introduces an obligation to submit electronic VAT returns, introduces an obligation to submit electronic summary information only for monthly periods, introduces joint and several liability for carrying out certain transactions, including in the case of a representative starting business, and introduces tax sanctions for incorrect settlement of tax. The Package of Solutions also amends the Tax Code and Tax Order Act. The project was subject to a very short 10-day consultation, from September 23rd to October 3rd. Many questions have been raised by business organisations, as well as negative assessments highlighting the absoluteness of some solutions, the increase in bureaucracy, labour expenditure and, of course, the increase in the cost of adapting companies to new procedural requirements. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u19	Thank you very much, Member. I invite Mrs Małgorzata Zwierzan, Circle of Members of Free and Solidarni.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u20	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Act on VAT on goods and services and certain other laws. As the projectors point out, VAT is one of the most important sources of revenue due to its profitability for the state budget. If we want to act productively for society in both social and economic policy, it is necessary to introduce new regulations to improve the collection of this tax. In this draft law, applicants propose to introduce a number of solutions referred to as a package of sealing solutions. One of them is to extend the catalogue of goods and services covered by the reverse charge mechanism, mainly those particularly vulnerable to abuse in this area. In assessing this solution, it is important that the reverse charge mechanism is a used and effective instrument for combating tax fraud throughout the European Union. In addition, it is envisaged to introduce the possibility of verifying the registration of new VAT taxable persons. Depending on the results of this verification, where it appears that the entity in question does not exist or is not in contact with it, the head of the tax office will be able to refuse to register it. If a newly registered entity is recognised on the basis of established links with already registered entities who are in arrears with taxes that there is a risk that the entity concerned will also be in default of paying tax duties, it will be possible to make the registration of that entity subject to its registration deposit. Further solutions to improve the efficiency of VAT collection include: Restoration of VAT sanctions in the event that the taxable person is found not to be reliable in a complex tax return where he has shown the amount of the tax liability lower than the amount due, restricting the application of the refund of the tax difference in an accelerated 25-day period only to specific cases and extending the joint and several liability to the agent of the entity starting the business in the event of the tax arrears of that entity arising from its participation in unfair tax settlement with a view to the return of the property benefit. The regulations introduced by this Act have the opportunity to effectively counter the fraud of unfair taxpayers and thus only to increase the gap in the state budget resulting from these frauds. When assessing this project, it can be said that it is above all happy that instead of focusing on inventing new burdens for Polish taxpayers, applicants focus on sealing the system that is already in force. However, there is no disillusionment due to the continued functioning of VAT rates, which were only increased by the previous government for a period of 3 years and have been in operation for almost 6 years. However, I hope that the solutions introduced by this law will result in the desired results and that the additional budget revenue resulting from the improvement of VAT collection will allow for a reduction in its rates. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports the proposed project and requests that it be submitted to the committee for further work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

There is also the question of joint and several liability by Mr Jachnik. The very principle, the operation of joint and several liability, is not that each taxpayer is liable for the obligations of the other taxpayer without any conditions. The principle of joint and several liability is based on the assumption that this taxable person had to know about some illegal behaviour of the other taxable person who did not pay VAT. Therefore, it is not so. We can see how joint responsibility exists so far, for example in the fuel industry. We're not dealing with situations like this that completely ignorant, dishonest... In fact, these cases are certainly few when honest taxpayers are responsible for the tax obligations of someone, this fraud. This is entirely due to the accident, the unawareness. The solid responsibility is based on the belief that, in fact, the one who has to pay VAT for this dishonest taxpayer simply had to know about certain irregularities in the functioning of the other entity. And one last thing, that's the taxpayers' register. The VAT Register is available online on the website of the Ministry of Finance. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u43	Thank you very much, sir. We proceed with the examination of item 19 of the agenda: First reading of the parliamentary draft law on the amendment of the Personal Income Tax Act and the Corporate Income Tax Act (Print No 969). I ask Mr Sylvester Tulayev to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u44	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the group of Members, I have the honour to present a draft law amending the Personal Income Tax Act and the Corporate Income Tax Act. The project is essentially about two changes, two key issues. The first is to guarantee the right to take account of the tax-free amount in 2016 and 2017. The second change is a change in the tax rules for investment funds. Let us recall that the Constitutional Tribunal, in its judgment of 28 October, 3 days after the Law and Justice of Parliament's elections, ruled on the Article. 27. He said it was Art. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Act of 26 July 1991 on personal income tax in so far as it does not provide for a mechanism to correct the tax reduction amount, which guarantees at least minimum existence, is incompatible with Article. Article 2 and Article 2 84 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The provision referred to in Part I shall, to the extent indicated therein, expire on 30 November 2016. The Constitutional Tribunal shall have the same effect. From the judgment, it follows from this operative part that the Court has actually ruled that there is no legislation. At the same time, the Constitutional Tribunal postponed the loss in force until 30 November 2016. Therefore, the operative part of the judgment may raise doubts. on the effects it will have on the expiry of the deadline. The views are different. The first is that the judgments on non-constitutionality of the legislative omission concern, in fact, the non-constitutionality of the whole provision, in this case the entire Article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 act. On the other hand, when we take into account that the statement of non-constitutionality referred in fact to the part of the provision in so far as it regulates the tax amount available to taxpayers, it cannot be excluded that the loss of force in force will apply only to the tax-free amount and, consequently, taxable persons will be deprived of the right to deduct tax by the amount guaranteed by the current legislation. Therefore, in order to ensure the constitutional principle of legal certainty for taxable persons and, in particular, to guarantee the right to take account of the amount of tax free at the current rate, there is an urgent need for a legislative initiative laying down the rules for calculating income tax in 2016. In addition, given the increase in the amount of free money announced by the Prime Minister in the exposé... [[Voice from the room: In the first 100 days.]] ...up to 8,000. and ongoing work on the reform of the tax system in the field of uniform tax, which is planned to implement this announcement and which is planned to enter into force on 1 January 2018, it is appropriate to maintain the existing tax rules also in 2017. This work is under the direction of Minister Kowalczyk and, as I said, the entry into force of this announcement is planned for 1 January 2018. The second point raised in the proposed bill is that the tax rules of investment funds should be amended. This is a change in the Corporate Income Tax Act. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 We propose to amend the rules on taxation of investment funds by covering Polish investment funds operating under the Act of 27 May 2004 on investment funds, i.e. open investment funds and specialised open investment funds, an exemption in place of an entity exemption, including interest income, dividends and other income from participation in corporate profits, disposal of claims, currencies, shares and other securities, including derivatives of financial instruments or the exercise of rights arising therefrom. This amendment will also consist of an equivalent exemption for foreign mutual investment institutions from the Member States of the European Union or the European Economic Area which fulfil the characteristics which enable them to be considered equivalent to open-ended investment funds and specialised open-ended investment funds. The inclusion of closed investment funds in the Income Tax Act is another change in the taxation of investment funds. These changes, the three key ones I mentioned, will therefore not affect the scope of taxation of national open investment funds and specialised open and equivalent foreign investment funds reaching the indicated revenue, as I mentioned earlier, despite the change in the exemption formula. I would like to point out that unlike open investment funds, closed investment funds offer the possibility of redirecting the issue of certificates to a particular investor, matching the investment strategy to individual needs. The control of the investment objective of the fund and the determination of investment policy and the lack of investment restrictions allow primarily investments in foreign companies with characteristics identical to the Polish form of the limited partnership. The characteristics of closed-ended investment funds make these funds used to manage assets, in particular by taxpayers with significant capital to exploit economies of scale. The use of closed investment funds for tax optimization will be applicable if the total costs of maintaining the fund do not exceed the amount of tax that the investor would have to pay by investing outside the structure of closed investment funds. Therefore, only entities investing in amounts of several tens of millions of PLN can afford to create such a private investment vehicle. This results in the advantage of large taxpayers vis-à-vis small and medium-sized enterprises. This situation has a very negative impact on the competitiveness of smaller operators, weakening their market position. Assuming that the financial result of the closed investment funds would be similar to the corporate income tax base, the change in the rules on taxation of investment funds could result in an increase in the revenue of the public finance sector by around PLN 2.2.5 billion for taxation of the income of closed investment funds. However, the amendment would be neutral for other types of investment funds to the extent that they would continue to generate income covered by the proposed exemption. I would like to announce now that a certain amendment to the draft law is being considered, because, as I mentioned, the act aims to amend and extend the provisions of the Income Tax Act to all closed investment funds. But we're paying attention. for those companies which apply the principle I mentioned earlier concerning those negative activities related to the use of closed investment funds in order to optimize taxation. This will certainly still be analysed during the work of the Committee on Public Finances. [[Voice from the room: What is this?]] Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Act will enter into force on 30 November 2016. Until that day, as I mentioned at the very beginning, the Constitutional Tribunal postponed the loss of power of the Article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 the Personal Income Tax Act, with the exception of the amendments introduced in the Corporate Income Tax Act for which we propose that they enter into force on 1 January 2017. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Group of Members, I recommend that this bill be adopted. I would also ask that this bill be further examined in the Parliamentary Committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u88	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour as rapporteur to present the report of the committee that discussed the Senate amendments yesterday. The Senate has decided to introduce eight amendments into the text of the Act adopted by the High House. All Senate amendments are recommended by the committee. Literally a few comments about some of them. Amendment 1 seeks to ensure that the application for authorisation to pursue the occupation of the carrier is also subject to the obligation to attach a certificate of non-punishment so that it cannot be replaced by its own declaration and to avoid interpretation in the light of which it would be acceptable to use the said documents as an alternative, i.e. a certificate or declaration. In the second amendment, the Senate expressly clarifies that the consequence of issuing a conviction against a person carrying out road transport activities for certain offences is the adoption of an administrative decision which states the loss of the good reputation of the carrier. In the third amendment, the senators introduced the obligation to consult the Polish national organisation which brings together road carriers in the procedure for assessing good reputation. Amendments 4 and 7 delete the provision which amended the delegation for the Minister responsible for transport to issue an implementing act because those provisions are regulated by the relevant Commission Regulation. And I would draw attention to the last, the 8th amendment, in which the Senate decides to shorten the deadline for the implementation of the electronic register of road transport operators by one month, given that, as I reported earlier, the law adopted by the High Sejm was adopted with several years of delay. In the meantime, on 5 October, a judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union against Poland was handed down, in which it was found that Poland had failed to fulfil its obligations under the article. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 5 of the 2009 European Parliament Regulation, which laid down common rules for the occupation of road transport operator. So much for me. Thank you for your attention.

They concern: the obligation of future road carriers to submit together with the application for authorisation of the occupation of road transport operator a certificate of non-criminality, the loss of good repute by the carrier on the basis of an administrative decision issued after the issuing of a conviction against that transport carrier, the amendment of a delegation to the Minister responsible for Transport for the issue of an implementing act and the shortening of the limitation period applicable to all infringements laid down in the Regulation of the Commission of the European Union. The Senate amendment, which raised doubts among some of the members of the Infrastructure Commission, refers to the imposition on the body assessing the good reputation of the entrepreneur an obligation to consult a Polish organisation of national scope, bringing together road carriers, whose trader is a member and which has been operating for at least 3 years. The opponents of this solution call for carriers to sign up to organisations to which they do not belong. However, I agree with the justification of the senators that such verification of the conduct of the business to date can significantly affect the administrative decision issued. Given the delay that we have in proceding these regulations in relation to the EU commitment, it is also important to speed up the provisions on the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs by one month. Today we are adopting a law establishing the Eastern Cross, a distinction intended for people who saved Poles in Kresów I and II of the Republic in the years 1937 and 1959. This first date, 1937 is a Polish operation, it was carried out by the NKVD, it was the beginning of mass repression, which in the Soviet Union hit our compatriots, and then, after 17 September 1939, also hit Poles on the Kresy II of the Republic. The year 1959 is the end of the second repatriation, and in fact the expatization of Poles from Kresów. Remembrance of those people who often risked their lives to save our fellow citizens is our moral duty. This is the duty of the Polish state. Their actions were an act of courage, an example of Christian mercy, an act of humanity in those inhuman times. Today, when establishing this award, the Polish state will honor those who have made a heroic effort to help our fellow countrymen in these tragic moments. I think a lot of people in this room have met with people from the Kresów. I think many of us have heard dramatic stories about what happened in the East. In these stories there were very often simple, ordinary Russians, Ukrainians, Kazakhs or Uzbeks who helped and sometimes even saved the lives of Poles. These people will be honored today. This distinction will remind not only Poles, but also the nations in the East that we Poles remember that we Poles are grateful for the help we have shown to our fellow countrymen. This distinction will also prove that we Poles can remember the dramatic history, but without hatred, without the application of collective responsibility and blame for the crimes of entire nations and communities, and the superior feeling which accompanies us in this memory is gratitude for the brotherly help given to every Pole. High Seym! On the other hand, it is worth to say, on the occasion of this matter, that by establishing the Eastern Cross, we remind not only of these dramatic events, but also the history and heritage of the Kresów I and II of the Republic of Poland. It's very important. It's part of our Polish identity. Poles often showed empathy towards people and communities of nations who experienced persecution, pogroms, or organized repression. And very well, because it shows our empathy, our sensitivity and openness to others. But sometimes it was possible to get the impression that this empathy and this sensitivity were lacking towards ourselves, towards the Polish people. For a very long time because of the communist regime, then because of the misunderstood political correctness we did not say... [[This isn't a vote now?]] ...about Poles' Gehenna in the East in the 20th century. [[Isn't this voting time?]] We did not talk about the Polish operation, about Katyn, about the Ponars, about the Kuropaty, about genocide in Volyn. Today, please, the High House, we pay tribute to all those who risked their lives saving Poles in the East, secondly, we restore the memory of the legacy and legacy of the entire Kresów, and thirdly, we are creating an important tool for historical policy. Therefore, I ask the High House to support the law on the establishment of the Eastern Cross. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]]

These are the measures that are prepared in such a way that they are intended to meet the most basic, elementary needs of the services subordinate to the MMSWiA. However, in order for these services to be able to carry out their tasks effectively, they must continue to be modernised, and they must be modernised. The first modernisation programme was, as I am sure you remember, certainly remembered, introduced in 2007 under the leadership of Prime Minister Jarosław Kaczyński. In addition to these measures, which are foreseen in the following years, in the budget laws for the following years, it provided for PLN 6301 million of additional funds and had the expected results, although not to the extent that this could have happened, because the implementation of this modernisation programme took place mostly not in our governments, but in the governments of the PO-PSL coalition. There were various reservations about how it was implemented. The Supreme Audit Chamber also expressed various reservations, doubts and comments. When the programme was over - he was later extended for two consecutive years, it was originally a programme for 2007 and 2009 and then extended until 2011, the measures foreseen for its implementation were reduced - everyone considered, not only Law and Justice, because we were the originator of this programme to renew, so everyone felt that it was necessary. Years passed and such a program was not created until the end of the PO-PSL coalition. We have written this idea, which is the idea of a new program for modernising uniformed services, in the Law and Justice programme. And this project is being implemented right now. The government has adopted a draft law which establishes a new programme for the modernisation of uniformed services for the years 2017 to 2020 i.e. a four-year programme. In short, it concerns three areas. The first area is investments and renovations, the second area - the purchase of the necessary equipment, equipment, weapons, means of transport, means of communication, i.e. all those tools that the services need to perform their tasks well and effectively. The question is addressed to the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration. I ask Mr Dariusz Starzycki to ask a question.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u379	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I would like to address the Minister at the request of his fellow volunteer firefighters who, on the basis of their daily experience, have proposed to amend the Fire Protection Act of 24 August 1991. Chapter 5 of the Fire Protection Act talks about the powers of firefighters of fire protection units and members of voluntary firefighters. A large part concerns compensation, pensions or compensation for health or other damage that a volunteer firefighter may suffer as a result of participation in rescue or exercise activities. However, the Act does not take into account that the role of voluntary firefighters is not limited solely to direct rescue operations and exercises. In the event that all units of the State Fire Department go to a large fire-fighting operation, for the so-called. the operational security of the district shall be equivalent to the voluntary fire department, which shall be on standby to take action in the event of a local incident. Volunteer firefighters are then in action, but these are not direct rescue operations. In such circumstances, members of the TSO shall be deprived of part of the allowances, e.g. Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union, and in particular Article 21 thereof. In the light of the above, we propose that the Fire Protection Act include the wording of the new article in Chapter 5 as follows: The presence of members of voluntary fire services in readiness to take immediate rescue action at the request of the State Fire Department or the municipality should be treated as rescue operations. I therefore ask the Minister, "Is the Ministry planning to take legislative action in this area or to implement work on other provisions in the Act which will allow for the treatment of being ready to take immediate action at the place and time indicated by the State Fire Department or the municipality on an equal footing with rescue operations, the effect of which [[♪ The bell ♪]] will grant certain powers to CSR members? One way of financing could be to agree to the payment of a special-purpose reserve for the performance of its own crisis management tasks, which is created in the framework of a budgetary resolution in each municipality each year. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u380	Thank you. Please respond to the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration of Mr Jarosław Zieliński.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u381	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! There is a difficulty in such a conversation, because it is difficult for us to write a bill in such a way or amend it. There is a proposal for a provision, and now we are to address it in a very urgent, rapid manner and to say whether it is possible, it should be done or not. So if you'll excuse me, my answer will be a little more general, not so specific, because it really requires analysis. But let's start over. To remind you of what we all know, but maybe it's worth noticing: Volunteer firefighters, which fulfil a very important role in fire protection in Poland, in the rescue and fire protection system, are associations. Associations in Poland work very much. These are also associations that operate among other associations. This fact is worth considering, because sometimes there are postulates that concern this association, and other associations can also ask for it, so this context is also important - of course not in the sense that we are talking about today, but for example. When it comes to the question of an allowance to a pension, such contexts arise. The fact that these are associations means that they are independent entities that are voluntary, also as regards the activity of people who implement their need to participate in such organisations. The Fire Protection Act of 24 August 1991 confers different types of powers on TSOs. These are in particular the cash equivalent for participation in rescue operations or fire training organised by the State Fire Department or the municipality and the monetary compensation where a member of the voluntary fire department in connection with participation in rescue operations or exercises has suffered injury to his health as a result of an accident. It shall be granted where a member of the TSO has not retained the right to remuneration or has not received sickness or rehabilitation benefits under separate rules during the period of incapacity for work. On the other hand, where he has received such benefits, but less than compensation, he shall be entitled to compensation up to the amount of compensation. In addition, voluntary firefighters also have other powers. They are related to the damage suffered by the firefighter in connection with participation in rescue operations or exercises. They may include one-off compensation in the event of permanent or long-term injury, a pension for total or partial incapacity for work and damage to property. The legislator in this law also secured members of the firefighter's family who died in connection with their participation in rescue operations, granting members of his family the opportunity to obtain one-off compensation for the death of a volunteer firefighter, a survivor's pension or compensation for damage to property. All these powers relate primarily to participation in rescue operations. It follows from the above that the provisions in force sought to protect as comprehensively as possible the members of the voluntary fire brigade. The necessary legislative intervention area was considered to be the situation in which both financial and health damage was incurred by a volunteer firefighter, but precisely in connection with rescue operations. Appreciating the merits of the associations, such as voluntary firefighters, for the local community and their participation in ensuring security in the area concerned, the proposal to introduce the provision of the Article. 28a to the Fire Protection Act, which consists in qualifying as rescue operations to remain ready for voluntary firefighters to take immediate rescue action at the request of the State Fire Department or the municipality, has been subjected, as far as possible in a short period of time, to a preliminary analysis, especially by the Chief of the State Fire Service. As a result of this preliminary analysis, it was concluded that it was worth considering this issue, but rather in the context of other possible solutions that could concern members of the voluntary fire department. It is not possible, however, to adopt the proposed solution in the proposed form because of its too broad and indeterminate nature. Payment entitlement e.g. the equivalent or other benefits associated with participation in rescue operations with a broad provision of the proposal for the wording of the Article. 28a, in the absence of a definition of being ready, defining what it is to be ready, would allow too many situations - too many and not defined - to be qualified as rescue operations. The financial costs of implementing such a solution, which cannot be estimated at present because it is not defined, could prove to be too high and, according to the proposal, the current way of financing the units of TSOs would remain unchanged, so an increase in the costs associated with their maintenance would significantly burden the municipality. Therefore, it will now be monitored, monitored by the management of the MMSWiA at the State Fire Department's Headquarters, and work will be carried out on a proposal for possible amendments to the Fire Protection Act in the area of this issue, namely the powers of members of voluntary fire services. Following a deeper analysis, after considering this, after the presentation of the project, of course, it will be possible to get acquainted with this project if it were to be addressed to the Parliament's work before this stage, i.e. after its publication in the framework of the government legislative process, and to participate in shaping its content also by the environment. [[♪ The bell ♪]] the most interested, i.e. voluntary firefighters, with whom we have excellent contacts and we have a permanent dialogue, by submitting possible comments and proposals in the course of a public consultation. But all of this requires a very deep thought, Mr President, and in advance I say that in this form, as proposed, this is difficult to accept.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u520	Thank you very much, sir. This statement concludes the examination of this item on the agenda. We proceed with the examination of item 23. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending certain laws in connection with the creation of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration (Print No. 930). I would like to ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Interior and Administration of Mr Sebastian Chwałk to present the justification for the bill. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u521	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Members, I would like to present a draft law amending certain laws in connection with the creation of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration. It is a cleaning project aimed at updating the names of the State Sanitary Inspection of the Ministry of the Interior and the Board of Housing Resources of the Ministry of Home Affairs, units previously subordinate to the Minister of Home Affairs and now the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration. To this end, the project provides for appropriate changes to the basics for the above mentioned. organisational units of legal acts, i.e. in the Act of 14 March 1985 on the State Sanitary Inspection, which defines the area of operation of the State Sanitary Inspection of the Ministry of Interior, and in the Act of 22 June 1995 on the accommodation of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, as well as in the Act of 10 July 2015 on the Agency for Military Property, governing the tasks of the Board of Housing Resources of the Ministry of Internal Affairs. In addition, the project also envisages the necessary amendments to other legal acts of the statutory rank, which include regulations relating to the State Sanitary Inspection of the Ministry of Interior, the Chief Health Inspector of the Ministry of Interior or the State Health Inspector of the Ministry of Interior, e.g. in the Pharmaceutical Law Act or in the Public Health Act of 11 September 2015. The draft also provides for the amendment of the Law of 4 September 1997 on the departments of government administration, consisting of the separation in the section: Information on infrastructure and spatial information, and the amendment in the Act of 4 March 2010 on the infrastructure of spatial information, in which he was replaced by the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. IT, administration as responsible for coordinating the establishment, maintenance and development of spatial information infrastructures has been designated as the Minister responsible for Spatial Information. It's computerization. The argument in favour of this solution is that the infrastructure for spatial information understood as the spatial data sets described by the methods, as well as their services and technical measures, processes, procedures used and made available by the relevant authorities are relevant to the scope of the minister responsible for the operation. IT, i.e. with the computerisation of public administrations and bodies performing public tasks, with systems, ICT networks of public administrations, supporting investment in information technology, the development of electronic services. Consequently, according to the project, in place of the existing geoportal of spatial information infrastructure of the main surveyor of the country, the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs was appointed. information provision in the performance of infrastructure tasks in the field of spatial information. I request that the House accept the further work of the project. Thank you.

High Seym! This generates various types of problems, above all the problem of increasing costs of living in prisons. The employment rate of prisoners in Poland is one of the lower in Europe. In Western European countries it reaches 60%. In our country, according to the Ministry of Justice, this rate has increased since the beginning of 2016 from 35% to 42%, currently working almost 28 thousand. Inmates. In accordance with the current wording of the Implementing Penal Code, no remuneration shall be payable for the cleaning and auxiliary work carried out in favour of the Prison Service's organisational units or for the cleaning work for the benefit of the local government of up to 90 hours per month. After the planned amendment, this provision is to be supplemented, inter alia. about work for social purposes for entities for which the authority of the municipality, district or voivodship is the body of the founding, state or local organisational units and companies governed by commercial law with the exclusive participation of the State Treasury, the municipality, the district or the voivodship. Social work will also be possible for institutions or organisations representing the local community and educational institutions, youth educational centres, youth sociotherapy centres, medicinal entities within the meaning of the provisions on medical activities, social assistance organizational units, foundations, associations and other institutions or public utility organisations providing charitable assistance. This means significantly increasing the number of entities for which prisoners could carry out work free of charge. In turn, the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights estimates that the proposal is a step towards rejecting the code rule, according to which the employment of inmates is paid. As she recalled, now only 40% Inmates shall carry out work of a paid nature. At this point, we should ask whether we should also look for solutions to increase the ability of prisoners to take paid work instead of merely expanding the number of persons entitled to work free of charge. An attempt to respond to this challenge is to increase the financial capacity of the Convicted Professional Activation Fund and the Industrial Development of Labor Works by increasing from 25% to 45% deductions for this purpose from the remuneration of the convicted person. At the same time with 10% up to 7% the reduction of deductions from this remuneration for the harmed and Post-Penitentiary Assistance Fund. Released in this way 3% remuneration is intended to be used for the professional activation of persons deprived of freedom. This solution should encourage businesses to employ the convicted more than they have done so far. The additional funds of the fund are also intended to be used for the construction of production infrastructure in the penitentiary units. Doubts about reducing prisoners' wages should be solved by international statistics. Poland as one of the few countries currently offers 100% of residents minimum wage for work, while in France or the UK it is 44%, while in Sweden and Hungary it is about 1/3 of the minimum wage. A higher deduction to the Professional Activation Fund will leave the wages of prisoners at an even higher level in relation to the minimum wage than in those countries. In the opinion of our club, the projects discussed today will increase the number of prisoners working for the benefit of themselves, as well as those entitled to work in prison. The project should also have a positive impact on the costs of maintaining the penitentiary system in Poland. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of sending the submitted draft to the committee for further work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u549	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The two draft laws presented aim to increase employment in establishments among those deprived of freedom. In recent years, people employed in closed facilities work less in prisons than they did a number of years ago. What affected it? He influenced it, among other things. the judgment of the Constitutional Court which decided in 2010 that the minimum wage in prison should be as much as the minimum wage outside the prison. However, this decision of the Constitutional Tribunal, based on humanitarian considerations, has led to the fact that the attractiveness of prisoners' work to commercial enterprises has decreased significantly. While more than 2/3 of the prisoners employed were paid in 2010, only 40% in 2015 It worked for a fee. This figure, in general terms, decreased from 17,000. to 10,000, while the number of employees increased greatly free of charge - from 8 000. Up to 14,000. Both the Council of Europe and the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights, as well as various studies indicate that working in prison is useful and useful. It sustains the prisoner's ability to earn, to work systematically. Also psychologically, it works well for him, because he has a variety of lives. He does not have to spend 23 of 24 hours in a cell where he has several meters at his disposal, often below even the statutory 3 m. It works against social exclusion, finally allowing the prisoner to pay for some of his own needs, to have the resources for his own needs, to pay off his obligations or to help his family. The proposals presented here aim to increase the funds that will be deducted from prisoners' wages into the prison activation fund, which in particular allows for a lump sum return for the entrepreneur who employs the prisoner, as well as for the construction of, for example. Some halls where prisoners could work, or training prisoners. This involves a certain danger, namely whether we can reduce the minimum wage in this way, because it is actually below the minimum wage. In this case, and in the explanatory memorandum of the Constitutional Court, and in other materials, the answer is that the minimum wage outside prison is intended to ensure minimum living conditions, while in prison prison the prison has these minimum conditions, which is more than just a minimum wage which is already paid by the State. Therefore, such deductions, all the more for the professional activation of other prisoners, seem justified. At the same time, this law extends the scope of free work. Until now, work free of charge could only be carried out in order, within the context of the needs of the prison or for the local government. This extends here to any actually state-owned facilities, including commercial law companies owned by the State Treasury. So we're starting to get quite dangerously close to a certain boundary, I'd say, slave labor, forced labor. It will be possible to hire prisoners. free of charge e.g. for works in state mines, if there is 100% state capital there. Here, we would already enter the area, I would say, contrary to the recommendations of international conventions, so I think that this issue should be carefully looked at so that this extension of free work does not violate the prohibition of slave labour and actually falls within the framework of non-commercial works, not for profit, but for the public good, for public purposes in fact. The sum of these deductions currently exceeds 50%. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u552	Małgorzata Zwierzan will now speak on behalf of the Free and Solidarni wheels. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u553	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the Act on the Employment of Freedomless Persons and the Law on Criminal Enforcement Code. As the projectors rightly point out, taking up work by convicts and prisoners in penitentiary units is the most effective form of their rehabilitation, since it facilitates the return to life at large and contributes to the reduction of the likelihood of recidivism in some prisoners by maintaining work-related habits, such as punctuality and systemicity. Aware of the positive impact of the work on inmates both during and after the penalty, applicants took a number of measures to increase employment among those deprived of freedom. According to the guidelines of the projectors, the employment of convicted persons decreased significantly after the increase in their remuneration, which was dictated by the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal in 2010, ordering them to be set at the level of the statutory minimum wage. Since then, despite the introduction of a number of mechanisms to counter the downward trend in convicted employment, the level of this employment has still not reached the pre-2010 ceiling and is still far from that ceiling. It is therefore considered necessary to step up efforts to increase the level of employment among persons in penitentiary units. The amendment of the Law provides for changes in the Penal Code which extends the scope of work carried out by convicted persons free of charge, increases the amount of the deduction paid to the occupational activation fund and the development of incarceration establishments from 25% to 45% the remuneration of the sentenced person and the reduction from 10% to 7% of the part of the remuneration of the sentenced person transferred to the Fund for the Victims and Post-Penitentiary Assistance. Projectors predict that these regulations will significantly contribute to the achievement of the main objective of the amendment. Increasing the financial capacity of the Convicted Professional Activation Fund and the Development of Incarceration Works will increase the number of paid jobs for inmates and thus increase the number of inmates earning to cover the financial penalties and maintenance imposed on them. The reduction of deductions to the Fund for Hurted Aid and Post-Penitentiary Assistance should have the same effect. In addition, broadening the scope of the work carried out by convicted persons free of charge by creating more posts will enable them to take up the work of convicted persons who are currently unable to do so. The amendment of the Law on the Employment of Freedomless Persons provides for the establishment of a lump sum for entrepreneurs for increased employment costs for those deprived of freedom at 40%. the value of the remuneration, which is an increase compared to the current lump sum by 20%. I share the opinion of the designers that increasing the attractiveness of the employment of penitentiaries, among others. by bringing the employer's costs closer to those before 2010, it will have a positive impact on the employment rate of inmates. In view of the positive impact of the employment of penitentiaries on their lives and the limited financial capacity to provide them with paid work, I accept the arguments of the applicants and the validity of the solutions they propose. The Free and Solidarni Circle is in favour of further proceedings of the law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u581	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft Maritime Equipment Act aims to implement the provisions of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 July 2014 on marine equipment, while repealing Council Directive 96/98. The project contains many references to the Law of 13 April 2016 on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems, so its process could only start after the publication of the Act. The scope of the regulation of the draft law contains basically the same elements as those laid down in the existing Maritime Equipment Act implementing the Council Directive of 20 December 1996 on marine equipment. In view of a number of new developments in the maritime equipment market surveillance sector introduced by Directive 2014/90, such as the establishment of requirements for marine equipment by means of implementing acts of the European Commission, the codification of the obligations of economic operators, the modification of the rules governing situations where derogations from requirements are permitted and, above all, the adaptation of the sanction catalogue to the new concept contained in the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems, instead of amending the existing law, it is advisable to adopt a new law, as we kindly ask. The draft law lays down the rules for carrying out the conformity assessment of marine equipment, the obligations of economic operators, the rules and procedures for authorising conformity assessment bodies of marine equipment, and the manner in which those bodies are notified and the functioning of the maritime equipment market surveillance system. The method of marking marine equipment provided for in the draft law does not differ in substance from the existing rules. The manufacturer's obligations shall primarily concern the drawing up of technical documentation, the carrying out of the conformity assessment procedure, the drawing up of the EU declaration of conformity and the application to marine equipment of the conformity mark. The draft law also sets out the obligations of the importer and distributor. The project grants the directors of maritime offices the role of maritime equipment control bodies either ex officio or at the request of the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. In this respect, the draft law makes extensive use of the system solutions contained in the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems. As regards the catalogue of sanctions for non-compliance with the proposed law, a significant change has been introduced. So far it has been a criminal responsibility. In order to strengthen the action of the proposed law and thus protect consumers and the environment, administrative penalties imposed by decision are proposed. The system of penalties in the draft law was based on the solutions adopted in the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems. Penalties will be imposed on an economic operator and their amount has been fixed in quota terms. The proposed law, depending on the act, sets a maximum penalty of 100,000. zł. Once the existing Maritime Equipment Act has been repealed, the existing implementing provisions under Article will apply to the type of marine equipment subject to conformity assessment procedures and to the list of international provisions containing requirements for marine equipment. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the repealed Maritime Equipment Act on Equipment Requirements - pending the entry into force of implementing acts adopted by the European Commission laying down such requirements. The proposed rules will not result in financial burdens on the state budget. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u582	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Janusz Śniadek will be the first to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u583	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the draft Law on Maritime Equipment, print No. 951. The draft shall lay down the rules for carrying out the conformity assessment of marine equipment, the obligations of economic operators, the rules and procedures for the authorisation of conformity assessment bodies of marine equipment and the manner in which those bodies are notified and the functioning of the maritime equipment market surveillance system. In addition to the elements existing in the existing Law of 20 April 2004 on marine equipment, the proposal contains new solutions provided for in Directive 2014/90, including the establishment of requirements for marine equipment directly by means of implementing acts of the European Commission and the inclusion in a single legal act of obligations of operators operating in the maritime equipment sector. The bodies carrying out the inspection of marine equipment, either ex officio or at the request of the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, will be directors of maritime offices, as is currently the case. The draft law also standardises derogations from the applicable requirements for marine equipment in the event of specific cases, e.g. the use of new technologies, trials or assessments of marine equipment, the need to exchange marine equipment on board a vessel at a port of a third country, the lack of availability on the marine equipment market bearing a mark of conformity. Implementation in Polish law of current EU requirements will establish conditions for Polish manufacturers to manufacture marine equipment in accordance with applicable standards, improve supervision of notified bodies and allow them to conduct competitive activities in this field on the European Union market. The provisions of the draft law will also ensure that administrative authorities effectively carry out the tasks of market surveillance of marine equipment. The proposed rules will avoid imposing inadequate burdens and obligations on economic operators, notified bodies and government administrations in the maritime equipment sector and will not result in financial burdens on the state budget, as the Minister said. It should be pointed out that the economic sector in Poland, to which the provisions of the Act will apply, is about 60 manufacturers of marine equipment operating in our country as well as exporting their products in the European Union. Those operators shall produce emergency equipment, navigation equipment or fire-fighting equipment that is so important to ensure the safety of navigation and the protection of the marine environment. Thanks to the proposed regulations, shipbuilding operators and ship owners will have access to the production in Poland and meeting international standards of marine equipment. This will certainly contribute to the development of this sector by ensuring the conditions for the maritime equipment sector in our country on competitive terms, while guaranteeing the highest technical safety requirements for the production and subsequent operation of marine equipment products. Mr. Marshal, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I request that this project be directed to further work. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u587	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle on the governmental draft law on marine equipment. The draft Maritime Equipment Act aims to implement the provisions of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on marine equipment. There are not many changes to previous regulations. The proposed changes are mainly linked to new developments in the maritime equipment market surveillance sector, which result inter alia. the need to codify the obligations of operators, to modify the rules governing situations where derogations from requirements are permitted. An important change is the indication of the power for the European Commission to define requirements for marine equipment in implementing acts. To date, these requirements have been included in the Annex to the Directive of the Council of the European Union. The Act sets out the criteria to be met by notified bodies for carrying out the evaluation and maintains the obligation of the Minister responsible for maritime affairs to carry out inspections of those bodies at least every 2 years. The way of marking marine equipment has been left unchanged, but only the design of signs has been included in the bill itself. The Act sets out the manufacturer's obligations concerning, in particular, the drawing up of technical documentation of marine equipment, the carrying out of the conformity assessment procedure, the drawing up of the declaration of conformity of the European Union and the placing on the equipment of the marine conformity mark. The project under investigation for failure to comply with the provisions laid down by law provides for administrative penalties to be imposed by decision. The draft law sets out the methods for calculating the penalty, its maximum amount and the time limit for payment of the penalty. The limitation of penalties has also been regulated. It is important that Navy, Border Guard, Police and Customs vessels are excluded from the scope of this Act and its provisions apply only to other ships of the European Union. I am very pleased that the draft law has been consulted with more than 20 stakeholders whose comments have been partly taken into account in the draft. Due to the need to align Polish laws with European law, the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle requests that the law be further proceded. Thank you very much.
