Elements that you can object to are e.g. the fact that the Act clearly does not specify the requirements of language documentation in Polish and English. It may be felt that each manufacturer applying for authorisation should prepare the dossier in all the languages of the EU Member States. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 9 we read that the manufacturer ensures that the EU declaration of conformity is drawn up in Polish and English. Where marine equipment is placed on board, a copy of the EU declaration of conformity in the Polish and English languages shall be kept on board the vessel until the marine equipment concerned has been removed from the ship. From the above it can be interpreted that, regardless of the Member State where the authorisation is obtained, the Polish language version is also in force. Also in the article. 11 we read that the manufacturer joins the marine equipment, drawn up in Polish and easily understandable for users, instructions and information concerning the safe installation of marine equipment on board the ship and its safe use, including information on possible operating restrictions, together with other documentation required in accordance with international regulations. Again, if this is to apply to equipment authorised in another EU country, it is difficult to organise instructions in Polish. In conclusion, if the interpretation of the law is such that permissions obtained in other countries are sufficient, and we may have documentation for them in English, there are no reservations from our point of view. If a Polish version is needed, it will be difficult and this will also be highlighted by the practitioners we have consulted by examining this document. With regard to the article. 16, you can say that all consent is always troublesome. In practice, there is no time to wait for an administrative decision, which, as we well know, never lasts for a short time when a ship can be stopped by a PSC type control body. Therefore, if the device or equipment is authorised for use by the port/state administration, temporary authorisation should be granted for its use and then an organisation authorised in the EU/Poland could give an authorisation certificate. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u592	High Seym! Mr. Marshal! The proposed law will ensure uniform application of market surveillance rules on marine equipment. The project is technical in nature - it adapts Polish law to European Union legislation. The draft law sets out the rules for carrying out the conformity assessment of marine equipment and the obligations of its manufacturers. The rules and procedures for the authorisation of conformity assessment bodies for marine equipment and the manner in which they are notified and the functioning of the market surveillance system for marine equipment are indicated. Implementation in Polish law of current EU requirements will create conditions for Polish manufacturers to produce marine equipment in accordance with applicable standards and allow them to conduct competitive activities on the European Union market. Supervision of notified bodies will also improve. The solutions contained in the draft law will ensure that administrative authorities effectively carry out the tasks of market surveillance of marine equipment. The proposed rules will avoid imposing inadequate burdens and obligations on economic operators, notified bodies and government administrations in the maritime equipment sector. The proposed provisions will replace the existing Law of 20 April 2004 on marine equipment. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of referring the project to the relevant committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u593	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! The proposal for a Maritime Equipment Act, hereinafter referred to as "the draft law', aims to implement the provisions of Directive 2014/90/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 July 2014 on marine equipment and repealing Council Directive 96/98/EC, hereinafter referred to as "Directive 2014/90'. The scope of the regulation of the draft law contains essentially the same elements as those laid down in the existing Maritime Equipment Act implementing the previous Directive, i.e. Council Directive 96/98/EC of 20 December 1996 on marine equipment. The draft law refers to the Law of 13 April 2016 on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems, which was constructed in a similar way to the previous reference to the Law on conformity assessment. In recent years, among others, it has been introduced. by means of implementing acts of the European Commission, a number of new solutions in the maritime equipment market surveillance sector introduced by Directive 2014/90 (setting requirements for marine equipment by way of a codification of the obligations of economic operators, modifying the rules governing situations where derogations from requirements are permitted) and, above all, the catalogue of sanctions has been adapted to the new concept contained in the Law on Conformity Assessment and Market Surveillance Systems. It is therefore appropriate to adopt a new law instead of amending the existing law. According to Art. 2 The proposed law applies to marine equipment which is placed or intended to be placed on EU vessels, while vessels of the Navy, Border Guard, Police and Customs are excluded from its scope. Scope of definitions set out in Article. In principle, 3 of the draft law does not differ from the definitions contained in the existing Maritime Equipment Act. It also replicates a number of other solutions that are contained in the current law. An important novelty compared to existing regulations is the power for the European Commission to define such requirements in implementing acts (signed in Article). 36 draft law). To date, these requirements have been included in the implementing acts. The situation with Article is similar. 6 a draft law which states the obligation for a notified body to carry out conformity assessment procedures, which are detailed in Annex 2 to the proposed law (so far the conformity assessment procedures were annexed to the Regulation on requirements for marine equipment). With regard to the inspection of notified bodies, the obligation of the Minister responsible for maritime affairs to carry out checks on those bodies has been maintained at least every two years. A novelty - introduced by Directive 2014/90 - is the possibility for a representative of the European Commission to take part in such an inspection as an observer. An important novelty resulting from the content of the article. The 9th draft law is to specify the model for the EU declaration of conformity by reference to Annex 3. Further articles in their essence duplicate the existing regulations. However, a novelty is the fact taken into account in Article. 18 a draft law allowing the placing on a ship of marine equipment without a mark of conformity in the absence of availability on the market of the equipment which has such a marking. A major change has been introduced in the field of sanctions - the existing legal status provides for criminal liability for breach of the obligations set out in the Maritime Equipment Act. In order to strengthen the action of the proposed law (consumer and environmental protection), administrative penalties imposed by decision are proposed. Penalties will be imposed on the operator up to PLN 100,000 depending on the act. When determining the amount of fines, the Director of the Maritime Authority will take into account the degree and circumstances of the infringement of the provisions of the Act, the number of marine equipment not complying with the requirements put on the market, put into service or made available on the market, the prior infringement of the provisions of the Act and the cooperation with the market surveillance authority. It was also possible for the Authority to waive the penalty if the economic operator provides evidence of corrective action. The implementing rules adopted so far pursuant to Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the repealed legislation on requirements for marine equipment is to apply until the entry into force of the implementing acts adopted by the European Commission laying down such requirements. The short deadline for entry into force of the Act (after 14 days from the date of publication) is due to the need for urgent implementation of Directive 2014/90/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 July 2014 on marine equipment and repealing Council Directive 96/98/EC, which expired on 18 September 2016. The Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of this law.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u611	Mr. Marshal! Mr. Senator! Dear Opposite Guests! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I have the honour to present a position on the Senate proposal for a law amending the Act on anti-communist opposition activists and those oppressed for political reasons and some other laws. The Act on anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons entered into force in the fourth quarter of 2015. The entire legislative process of the Act lasted for years and the final effect was far from preliminary intentions. Let me just remind you that the debate on the form of recognition by the state of the merits of those who act and fight for Poland's independence began in 2000, but then the tone of this debate was given by Gazet Wyborcza, in which Adam Michnik wrote: we are not veterans of solidarity and we do not need any help, because our environment has settled well. Here I think he told the truth, because the environment of the Wyborcza Gazeta was very well placed in the Third Republic of Poland. [[Applause]] The reasons and effects of this setting are well known, so I won't talk about it. However, the previous coalition and parliament did not change much their perception of those who fought the communist dictatorship. She could not agree to the proposals of the anti-communist opposition and the oppressed. The unilateral view of the authorities, the lack of sufficient reflection and research on the life situation of the members and activists of the anti-communist opposition, members of their families, as well as ordinary citizens subjected to repression, including material ones, has led Parliament to adopt a solution based on an over-precise rule. This is a delicate term because the current law limits the possibility of granting a benefit, as a rule, only for a period of one year, taking into account the relevant income thresholds. The benefits are social aid, without prejudice to any type of repression, including those not covered by compensation proceedings, e.g. under-retirement or wages. The proposed bill meets the expectations of some of the activists of the widely understood anti-communist opposition. In addition to changes in the granting of assistance to opposition activists and repressed persons, it amends the definition of the person being repressed, including those being repressed during the period from 1 January 1956 to 31 July 1990, and extends the definition of persons being repressed, stating that repression could also be classified. This applies, in particular, to persons who have been prevented from being promoted because of opposition activities and those who have been expelled from work. It is particularly important to abolish the social nature of aid to opposition activists and oppressed persons. The project provides for the autonomous nature of the benefit granted, as is included in the Veterans' Act. The amount of the benefit is not satisfactory, but the project is a form of recognition of the status of those who fought for free Poland, regardless of the threat of repression, which is also important at the moment. Finally, the Polish state begins to correct errors and fulfil its obligation to citizens. We are aware of the budget constraints and that the needs are enormous, but we must stress with all our power: the amount of the benefit is far too low. They'll still get fewer victims than their hangovers. I know that the regulation of this issue goes beyond the scope of this law, so I can only remind you here that people are still waiting for justice in this regard. Given the reservation raised here, but believing that this is not the last action for the benefit of these people and that in the near future, as soon as the funds are secured in the budget, the High House will not hesitate to express a meaningful thanks and recognition to the silent heroes, thanks to whom today we sit in the parliament of free Poland, [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Free and Solidarni Circle is in favour of adopting the proposed changes as soon as possible. Finally, on behalf of my colleagues and myself, I would like to express my special appreciation for all those who have spared no effort in fighting for free Poland. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u640	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Group of Members, I have the honour to present to the High House a draft law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal, print No 963. The draft law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal is the second of the three acts planned to deal comprehensively with cases relating to that tribunal. The proposed law is intended to replace the Act of 22 July 2016 on the Constitutional Court. The project shall implement the disposition resulting from the article. 197 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The issues which are not directly covered by the concept of organisation or procedure have been excluded, and so the designers have deviated from the description of the subject matter of the Constitutional Tribunal and applied a new organisation and procedure before the Constitutional Tribunal. This is the implementation of the proposal of a panel of experts on matters of the Constitutional Tribunal appointed by the Marshal of the Sejm. The draft shall also take into account other recommendations resulting from the report of the panel of experts on matters of the Constitutional Tribunal, as well as certain recommendations set out in the opinions of the Venice Commission. In its 2016 Constitutional Court Act, the legislator failed to take into account all conclusions resulting from the opinions on the functioning of the Court. At the time of the adoption of this law, the conclusions of the panel of experts for the Constitutional Tribunal were not known. In this connection, the group of Members considered it necessary to draw up and prepare a new legislative act which would take into account the multi-monthly work of the panel of experts in the case of the Constitutional Tribunal and respond to the dispute concerning the organisation and procedure of the tribunal. This dispute has recently covered not only the standards concerning the procedure for the control of constitutionality of the law, but also the issues of the status and internal competences of the bodies of the Constitutional Tribunal. The draft law takes into account the previous regulations concerning the organisation and proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. The basic distinction in relation to the normative acts in force between 1997 and 2016 is that the law excluded the matter concerning the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, which was transferred to another law. The designer divided the bill into three departments. Section I: Organisation of the Constitutional Tribunal, Section II : Procedure before the Constitutional Tribunal, Section III : Final provision. The project contains both solutions known to the Constitutional Tribunal laws and new solutions. Above all, the institutions which have so far not been present in the Constitutional Court laws will be identified here. Section I defines the organisation of the Constitutional Tribunal. It consists of three chapters: General provisions, bodies of the Court, the Registry of the Court and the Office of the Legal Service of the Court; branch 1: Common provisions, branch 2: Registry of the Court, branch 3: Office of the Legal Service of the Court. New legal arrangements concerning the organisation of the tribunal include a new mechanism for the election of candidates for the position of President of the Constitutional Tribunal by the General Assembly. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 11 The designer has laid down the statutory conditions for presenting candidates to the general meeting for the positions of President and Vice-President of the Tribunal. Since various entities are involved in the process of filling these posts, the President of the Republic of Poland must also be the President of the Republic of Poland in addition to the general assembly. After the election of the candidates by the General Assembly, they shall be presented to the President, whose prerogative is the appointment of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Vice-President respectively. In this respect, the General Assembly shall adopt an appropriate resolution, which it shall forward to the President of the Republic of Poland. The General Assembly's resolution indicating a set of candidates for President of the Constitutional Tribunal shall be an expression of the action referred to in Article. Paragraph 194. 2 constitutions. The adoption of a resolution by a majority indicates that the majority of the members of the General Assembly agreed to present the person as candidates to the President of the Republic of Poland. The failure to adopt this resolution makes it impossible for the General Assembly to present candidates. Art. Paragraph 194. 2 of the Constitution does not make the President of the Republic of Poland a notary confirming the resolution concerning the presentation of candidates by the General Assembly. Neither the Constitution nor the Constitutional Tribunal Act indicate the criteria that the President would be guided by the appointment of the President or Vice-President of the Tribunal. The President's sole responsibility in this respect is to act on the basis and within the limits of the law. He may appoint the President of the Constitutional Tribunal only if the procedure for the submission of candidates by the General Assembly has taken place in accordance with the law. As proposed in the Article. 11 The mechanism for selecting candidates for the general assembly as President or Vice-President of the Tribunal does not in any way restrict the constitutional role of the President of the Republic of Poland in the appointment of these bodies, while respecting the right of the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal to present candidates for the President or Vice-President of the Constitutional Tribunal. This guarantees a constitutional balance of power. The proposed solution ensures the true representativeness of the choice made and takes into account its legitimacy and representative aspect. This is to be guaranteed by the requirement that the candidate obtains minimum support. The designer does not waive this requirement, even if the procedure for the general assembly to select candidates ends in failure. For this reason, it proposes repeating the vote and ultimately repeating the whole process of presenting candidates for the position of President or Vice-President of the Constitutional Tribunal. The idea is that, at the stage of the selection of candidates, they should be the judges of the tribunal with the support of more members of the general assembly. For these reasons, in the Article. 11 The mechanism shall always have at least one of the candidates of the general meeting presented for the post of President of the Constitutional Tribunal to receive at least five votes in the selection procedure. The new election mechanism for the President of the Constitutional Tribunal provides for the start of procedures for the appointment of the President or Vice-President only after the end of the term of office of the President-in-Office of the Constitutional Tribunal. From the date of the opening of the vacancy until the election of the President of the Tribunal, the Tribunal shall be chaired by a judge who has the longest period of work in total: an internship as a judge in the Constitutional Tribunal... [[It's best if she goes to martial law.]] ...come on as an applicant, asessor, judge of the general court and state administration at central level. This solution is intended to ensure the organisational continuity of the Court's operation. The appointment of the President or Vice-President of the Constitutional Tribunal after their term of office was recommended by a team of experts to the Constitutional Tribunal. [[A hero.]] According to Art. 10 [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: The two draft terms of office of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Vice-President respectively shall be six years, but shall not last longer than the end of the term of office of the Judge of the Tribunal appointed as President of the Tribunal or the expiry of the term of office of that Judge of the Tribunal before the end of his term of office or the expiry of the term of office of the President of the Tribunal. This term shall expire in the event of the death of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal or of the submission by a judge of a tribunal which is the President of the Tribunal, which for six months... is, in principle, obsolete, but to this day was still... until the age of 70, in accordance with the procedure laid down in separate regulations. The recommendation to introduce the term of office of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Vice-President included a team of experts for the Constitutional Tribunal in Recommendation No V. The draft assumes that the appointment of the President or Vice-President of the Tribunal is inadmissible. This approach corresponds to the position of the Constitutional Court in its judgment of 9 December 2015, e.g. K 35/15. The draft aligns the regulation on the position of the vice-president of the Tribunal with the actual role of that body. The Constitution does not confer any powers on this entity. The scope of the vice-president's competence therefore depends on the legislator. The draft underlines the ancillary nature of this position towards the President of the Tribunal and indicates a close link between these bodies. The scope of the tasks to be carried out by the Vice-President depends on the President of the Constitutional Tribunal. The proposed regulation is more appropriate to the position of vice-president in the organisational system of the tribunal. The proposed solution is similar to those in the Supreme Court and the Supreme Administrative Court. Namely, in the absence of the first President of the Supreme Court, he is replaced by the President of the Supreme Court appointed by him. The Rules of Procedure of the Supreme Court clarify in § Paragraph 7 1 that the Presidents of the Supreme Court are deputy to the first President of the Supreme Court in the area defined by him and direct the work of the chambers. Similarly, Vice-Presidents of the Supreme Administrative Court shall be Deputy President of the Supreme Administrative Court within the scope determined by the President. The President of the Tribunal may, in special situations, authorise another Judge of the Tribunal to exercise certain powers belonging to the President of the Constitutional Tribunal. The possibility of authorising another judge to exercise certain powers of the President stresses the collegiality of the Court and equal responsibility of all its members for the proper functioning of that institution. The draft provides for the adoption of an ethical code. This code is particularly important in determining the duties of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and disciplinary responsibility. Its issue is the implementation of the recommendation of the panel of experts to the Constitutional Tribunal. I stress that the adoption of the Code of Ethics is the competence of the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. They're the ones who'll draw up the code of ethics, and they're gonna make a resolution to adopt it. Art. The 15th draft clearly sets out the matter of the Rules of Procedure of the Constitutional Tribunal. This is an internal act. The way in which matter may be determined, which may be the subject of the Rules of Procedure, must take account of the fact that the act must not itself impose material or procedural obligations on parties to proceedings or operate the obligations imposed by the law on those entities, nor must it deprive them of their powers. The newly designed subsidiary of the Constitutional Tribunal is intended to provide efficient administrative services and reliable substantive assistance to the Tribunal and its judges. The Court's office is replaced by two organisational units, namely the Court's Registry and the Court's Legal Service Office. The appointment of these bodies is intended to improve organisational and administrative activities, rationalise employment and ensure the quality of the supporting apparatus of the Constitutional Tribunal. Changes in the organisational and administrative working conditions of the Court will allow for the elimination of unnecessary bureaucratic apparatus. The Court's law firm and the Office of the Court's Legal Service have been distinguished according to the criterion in question. Similar divisions within the organizational units occur in the Supreme Court, there is the Law Firm of the First President of the Supreme Court and the Office of Studies and Analysis of the Supreme Court. It is similar in the Supreme Administrative Court. It is the law firm of the President of the Supreme Administrative Court and the Office of the Court. The proposed solution therefore falls within the organisational standards in the highest courts of the Republic of Poland. The projector assumed that, as in the Supreme Court, the proceedings in matters of labour law against the employees of the organisational units of the tribunal are carried out by the President of the Tribunal or by a person authorised by him. The primary task of the Chancellery of the Court is to ensure the organisational and administrative conditions of the Court's work. The law firm is to consist of the director of the law firm appointed by the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, from the Administrative Department, i.e. staff employed in administrative positions, and from the Court Guard Department, i.e. staff employed in positions related to the protection of the Constitutional Tribunal. The tasks and structure of the firm in the field of the unregulated law are to be determined by the statutes of the firm. Detailed questions relating to the positions in the Court's Registry are to be determined by order by the President of the Court, after consulting the Director of the Court's Registry. The proposal provides for the adoption of the Rules of Procedure for the remuneration of the Court's Chancellery. The draft stipulates that the remuneration of the Director of the Court's Registry may not exceed the remuneration provided for by the Director of the Court of Appeal laid down in the implementing rules issued under the Law on the System of General Courts. In cases not governed by the Act, the provisions of the Act on employees of public authorities shall apply to the employees of the law firm. The task of the Office of the Court's Legal Service shall be to provide the substantive service directly related to the judicial activities of the Court and to assist in the work of the Court's judges. The Office of the Legal Service of the Court is to consist, firstly, of the Director of the Office of the Legal Service of the Court, appointed by the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, secondly, of the assistants of the Judges of the Tribunal, thirdly, of the Department of Preliminary Verification of the Scriptures initiating the proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal, fourthly, of the Faculty of Studies on the Court of Justice and Legal Analysis. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: The 26th draft found that the Assistant Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal is carrying out activities aimed at preparing cases for the tribunal's examination and administrative activities. The basic qualification requirements for assistants are: first, Polish citizenship and the exercise of full civil and civil rights, second, impeccable character, third, completed higher law studies in Poland and master's degree or foreign legal studies considered equivalent in Poland. These criteria are derived from Article. The 155th Law on the System of Universal Courts. This is the minimum appointed by the legislator to take up the post of Assistant Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. The project assumes that the selection of candidates takes place by means of a competition organised and conducted by the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, after consulting the Director of the Legal Service Office. Information on the competition must be included in the Public Information Bulletin of the Constitutional Tribunal. The order of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal is to determine: firstly, the number of assistants of the judges of the Tribunal, secondly, the additional qualification requirements, thirdly, the detailed scope of their tasks and the manner and conditions for their execution. The preliminary examination of the Scriptures initiating proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal is to assist the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in carrying out the tasks related to the preliminary review. The Faculty of Law Studies and Legal Analysis is intended to support the process of ruling in the Constitutional Court with a scientific consultation and carry out tasks relating to the legal compliance, consistency and uniformity of the case law of the Tribunal. The tasks and structures of both departments in the field of the unregulated law shall be determined by the statute of the office. The designer set out basic requirements for department staff. The basic qualification requirements for employees from both departments are: first, Polish citizenship and the exercise of full civil and civil rights, second, impeccable character, third, completed higher law studies in Poland and master's degree or foreign law studies considered equivalent in Poland. In addition, a member of the faculty must have at least a doctorate degree in law due to the nature of the tasks to be carried out within the faculty and their scientific and expert character. Issues such as the number and list of posts on which persons belonging to the departments are employed, other additional qualification requirements, the detailed scope of tasks in those posts and the manner and conditions of their execution have been left to the President of the Constitutional Tribunal. As in the case of the Chancellery of the Court, the drafter provided for the issuance of the Rules of Procedure for the remuneration of the staff of the Office of the Legal Service of the Court. The remuneration of the Director of the Office of the Legal Service of the Court shall not exceed the remuneration provided for by the Director of the Court of Appeal laid down in the implementing rules issued under the Law on the System of General Courts. According to Art. 32 projects in cases not covered by the Act shall be governed by the provisions of the Law on Officials of the Courts and the Public Prosecutors' Office. Basic legal arrangements for proceedings before the Tribunal: Section II governs proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. It consists of 10 chapters: jurisdiction of the Court; formations of the Tribunal; exclusion of a judge of the Court; participants of proceedings; procedural documents; costs of proceedings; course of proceedings before the Court; branch I - Initiation of proceedings; branch II - Dismissation of proceedings; branch III - Preliminary review of applications, legal questions and constitutional complaints; branch IV - Recognition of the case before the Constitutional Tribunal. Special provisions on proceedings by the Tribunal: branch 1 - Procedure for a constitutional complaint; branch 2 - Procedure for compliance with the Constitution of objectives or activities of political parties; branch 3 - Procedure for settlement of a dispute of competence; branch 4 - Procedure for finding an obstacle to the exercise of office by the President of the Republic of Poland; Judgments and sittings; Judgments of the Tribunal. The various chapters of Chapter II contain both common provisions relating to all procedures before the Constitutional Tribunal and those applicable only to some of them. With regard to proceedings such as: first, constitutional complaints, second, the examination of compliance with the constitution of the objectives or activities of political parties, thirdly, disputes of competence between central constitutional authorities of the State, fourth, the recognition of the interim obstacle of the President in the exercise of office, the designers in separate branches only dealt with those issues which differed from the others. On the other hand, issues concerning e.g. the content of procedural letters initiating proceedings, participants in proceedings, meetings and hearings, as well as decisions are laid down in chapters covering all proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. Chapter 1 of Chapter II governs the jurisdiction of the Constitutional Tribunal. Art. 33 of the project lists the cases in which the tribunal is adjudicated. This summary is based on the powers conferred on that body in the Constitution. The drafting of this provision refers to the article. 3 of the Constitutional Court Act of 2015 The proposed law has not amended the rule that the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code apply mutatis mutandis to matters not governed by the law to proceedings before the Tribunal. It's just like it used to be. As regards formations, the project retains the mechanism for determining the compositions of the hearing bodies before the Court of Justice in force under the Constitutional Tribunal Act of 2016. They shall take into account the conclusions of the discussions on the rules for the formation of the Constitutional Tribunal, including the conclusions of the opinion of the Venice Commission and the recommendations of the panel of experts on matters of the Constitutional Tribunal. The Court is to rule in full court, in the composition of 5 Judges and in the composition of 3 Judges. The criterion for the division of cases between different compositions is of a particular nature. It consists of the grading of cases dealt with by the composition depending on the type of act and the substance of the case under review. The draft law, as well as the 2016 Constitutional Court Act, maintains that the full composition requires the participation of at least 11 judges. The hearing in full shall be chaired by the President and, in the event of obstacles to the chairing, by the Judge appointed by him of the Tribunal. Chapter 4 of Section II provides for a collective agreement between the parties to proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. The provisions of this Chapter shall specify both the participants in the procedure for determining the conformity of normative acts with higher-ranking acts and other matters which may be the subject of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. The possibility of wide participation in the Ombudsman's proceedings was maintained, known from the previous law. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 43 and Article 43 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers The rules of operation of the representative or representative have also been laid down. In addition, in Chapter 4 to the list of participants to date, the designer added participants to the proceedings on the finding of an obstacle to the exercise of office by the President of the Republic of Poland. The draft also sets out the rules for the appointment of representatives in these proceedings. Chapter 5 of Section II broadly regulates the issues of pleadings before the Court, including the formal requirements that they must fulfil. In particular, the drafter drew attention to the letters initiating proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. In addition to certain editorial corrections and clarification of the wording, the solutions indicated refer to the previously existing Constitutional Tribunal laws, in particular the 2016 Court of First Instance Act. As a normative novelty, it should be pointed out that Chapter 5 separately sets out the formal requirements of the proposal, firstly, on the constitutional compatibility of the political party's objectives, secondly, on the constitutional compatibility of the political party's activities and, thirdly, on the resolution of a dispute of competence and on the finding of an obstacle to the exercise of office by the President of the Republic of Poland. To the known conditions of redemption, this related to the so-called. discounting requests from groups of Members or Senators. According to Art. 59 (1) (a) Point 1 (5) of the draft tribunal shall, at a secret meeting, issue a decision to discontinue proceedings in the event of the end of the term of office of the Sejm and the Senate in cases pending on the basis of a request from a group of Members or Senators referred to in Article. Article 191 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point 1 of the Constitution. In this way, long-term practice has been sanctioned. In addition, the proposal limits the possibility to rule on provisions which have become obsolete. This will only be possible if a ruling is necessary to protect constitutional freedoms and rights in the context of a constitutional complaint. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 67 The designer has clearly defined what is to be understood by the subject-matter of the appeal, the subject-matter of the check and the control pattern. In this respect, the Law refers to the expressions used in legal doctrine. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 64 of the project shall be governed by the principles of joint identification of applications, legal questions or constitutional complaints. The principle is to combine cases initiated in the same mode. The designer introduces a clear ban on combining cases initiated in different control modes. The project develops the issue of access to the file. Art. 73 of the project ensures access to the file in proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal and contains regulations on the protection of personal data. The solution is more extensive than in the 2016 Constitutional Court Act. [[How much more of this nonsense are we gonna listen to?]] Art. The 74th project set out the rules for reviewing the case file, which is also intended to express the exercise of the right to a fair procedure before the Court. To date, these issues have been detailed in the Rules of Procedure of the Court. The projector considered that they should be regulated by the law. The question of proceedings concerning the finding of an obstacle to the exercise of office by the President of the Republic of Poland. This competence of the Court was not extended by the 2016 Act. The drafter, by model of the 2015 Act on the Constitutional Court, therefore clarifies this issue and introduces the basic procedural rules in this respect. The draft, as in the 2016 Constitutional Court Act, makes it the principle of recognition of cases at the hearing. However, the possibility of hearing cases at classified meetings has been added. This institution was extended in the Constitutional Court Act of 2015. In addition to the possibility to hear the case in secret, where it is clear from the written views of all the parties to the proceedings that the normative act on the basis of which the court or public administration finally ruled on the applicant's constitutional freedoms or rights or obligations is incompatible with the constitution, a judgment without a hearing may also be given if, first, the written positions of all the parties to the proceedings and the other evidence gathered in the case constitute sufficient grounds for a judgment or, secondly, where the case concerns a legal matter which has been sufficiently clarified in previous rulings of the Constitutional Tribunal. The possibility to adjudicate at a secret meeting cannot be a principle. The proposer proposed that the necessary conditions for the hearing of the case should be the appointment of all participants in the proceedings. According to the project, cases cannot be identified at a secret meeting in the case of cases dealt with in full. The projector maintained the 30-day time-limit for the appointment of the hearing known from the Constitutional Court Act of 2016. This time limit may be reduced by half by the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, but with the possibility for the initiators to object in this matter. In contrast to the 2016 Act, the project does not provide for exceptions to the possibility of shortening the time limit within which the hearing is to take place. The designer has resigned from the appointment of hearings on applications in order of impact, which is in line with the recommendations of the Venice Commission. The draft clarifies the obligations of the participants in the hearing, which so far laid down the rules of procedure of the Court. In particular, rules related to the management of the break in the hearing have been identified. The drafter maintained the vote on the ruling by a majority. The previous arrangements for qualified majority have not been re-established. Such action is in line with the indications set out in the opinion of the Venice Commission. The draft also waives the possibility of objecting to four judges in a full session against the proposed decision. It is worth noting that, in this respect, We listened to the opposition's voice and the position of the Venice Commission. The designer varied the issue of public publication of a decision against a notice within the meaning of Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this In the latter case, he referred to the rule resulting from the Law on the publication of normative acts and certain other acts. The rule that the order of announcement is made by the President of the Constitutional Tribunal is restored. In order to make a judgment public, an act of an authorised entity is necessary. The issuing body of the Official Journal in which the decisions of the Tribunal are published may not act ex officio in this respect. The proposalor decided that, due to the new way of regulating the matter concerning the tribunal and the need to ensure consistency of the regulation, the transitional, adaptation and final provisions will be included in a separate law: introductory provisions. [[That's enough, really.]] Finally, the new law sets out in an orderly manner the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the tribunal, which is intended to enable the latter to operate more efficiently. This will be possible by strengthening the legitimacy of the General Assembly, in particular when presenting candidates for the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, and by clarifying and clarifying the procedure before the Constitutional Tribunal. In this way, those wishing to initiate proceedings will have the guarantee of a fair procedure, and the tribunal, within its independence and at the same time within the limits of the law, will be able to exercise its powers freely. It should be stressed that the fundamental changes in the shape of the regulation compared to previous laws concern primarily the organisation of the Constitutional Tribunal. The constitutional crisis related to the Constitutional Tribunal has shown that the shortcomings arising from the formation of the organisational structure of the tribunal, including the determination of the internal competences of the tribunal's bodies and the administrative apparatus, hinder the smooth operation of the institution. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-04-sejm-29-2.u642	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The Act is also the implementation of the conclusions and indications of a team of experts appointed by the Marshal of the Sejm, which indicated the separation of the matter concerning the Tribunal into three legal acts - the act concerning the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, the act concerning the procedure and organisation of proceedings before the Court and the implementing rules. You can also tell more about this law. It largely responds to the conclusions of the Venice Commission's opinion, but also to the opposition. By introducing the criteria for the election of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal in the 2016 Act, the opposition protested against three candidates who could have little support, even one vote, among the judges of the Tribunal. The current draft assumes that the candidates will be those judges who have received at least five votes. The Act also points to the six-year term of office of the President, which offers a chance for a greater rotation as President, which can only have positive effects in the non-orthodox scope of the Court's management. The proposal excludes the possibility for three judges to apply for a full court in a given case, which, undoubtedly, also by the votes of the opposition, can only result in an acceleration of proceedings. The principle of dealing with cases has been waived by order of impact. I remember in this regard, for example, Mr Budka's statements, which indicated that queuing cases according to the date of impact would cause that the laws of this term of office of the Sejm would not be able to be audited. Ladies and gentlemen, in this situation, surely this prophetic prophecy of Mr Budka will not work. The General Assembly was also authorised to introduce a code of ethics for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. Today, for example, during the debate on the Statute of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, several times have been asked who will determine these principles of ethics. Let me remind you that this project, although today is the first reading, was published on the pages of the Sejm several days ago. So we could see that the powers in this regard would be proposed to the general assembly. The Act also provides for a much-needed solution to indicate who will be the head of the tribunal between the expiry of the current President's term of office and the election of another by the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. It should also be pointed out that the law uses a number of solutions that the applicant Member has pointed out, already known from the Supreme Court Act. I'm summing up. The Law and Justice Club will support the project and ask for it to continue. This will also be directed against them by this act of closing the school, because it is they who become ambassadors of Polishness. Finally, every school is such a Polish house, this is the place where Poland is spoken about, where Poland is promoted, and in this school even a festival of Polish films was created not only for the Polish community, but for the entire American community, so the loss will be huge. We must do everything to ensure that our diplomatic services, even if they have been there recently, show more commitment and heart for this matter, for this school and for Polonia. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
